Om lov om linjekonferanser m.v. Annex I to Council Regulation (EEC) No. Vedlegg 1. Forbehold 954/79 Reservations Når medlemsstater ratifiserer eller tiltrer konvensjonen, skal de ta de tre følgende for behold og fortolkningsforbehold: When ratifying the Convention or when acceding thereto, member states shall enter the following three reservations and inter pretative reservation: 1. I forhold til konvensjonen kan begre pet «nasjonal linje» med hensyn til en av medlemsstatene i Det Europeiske Økono miske Fellesskap omfatte ethvert linjerederi etablert i samsvar med EFØ-traktaten på vedkommende medlemsstats territorium. 1. For the purposes of the Code of Conduct, the term national shipping Une may, in the case of a member state of the Community, include any vessel-operating shipping line established on the territory of such member state in accordance with the EEC Treaty. 2. (a) Without prejudice to paragraph (b) of this reservation, Article 2 of the Code of Conduct shall not be applied in conference trades between the member states of the Community or, on a reciprocal basis, be tween such states and the other OECD countries which are parties to the Code. 2.a) Med forbehold for denne paragrafs punkt b), skal konvensjonens artikkel 2 ikke anvendes på konferansefart mellom med lemsstatene i Det Europeiske Økonomiske Fellesskap, eller på grunnlag av gjensidighet mellom slike stater og de andre OECD-land som har tiltrådt konvensjonen. b) Bestemmelsene under punkt a) skal ikke påvirke mulighetene for utviklings lands linjer, som er anerkjent som nasjonale linjer i samsvar med konvensjonen, til å delta som tredjelandslinjer i slike trafikkom råder i overensstemmelse med prinsippene i konvensjonens artikkel 2, forutsatt at disse linjer (b) Point (a) shall not affect the opportun ities for participation as third-country ship ping lines in such trades, in accordance with the principles reflected in Article 2 of the Code, of the shipping lines of a developing country which are recognized as national shipping lines under the Code and which are: i. allerede er medlemmer av en konfe ranse som opererer i disse trafikkområder, eller (i) already members of a conference serv ing these trades; or ii. blir opptatt i en slik konferanse etter reglene i konvensjonens artikkel 1.3. (ii) admitted to such a conference under Article 1 (3) of the Code. 3. Artiklene 3 og 14.9 i konvensjonen kom mer ikke til anvendelse i konferansefart mellom medlemsstatene i Det Europeiske Økonomiske Fellesskap, eller på grunnlag av gjensidighet mellom slike stater og de andre OECD-stater som har tiltrådt kon vensjonen. 3. Articles 3 and 14 (9) of the Code of Con duct shall not be applied in conference trades between the member states of the Community or, on a reciprocal basis, be tween such states and the other OECD countries which are parties to the Code. 4. I trafikkområder hvor artikkel 3 i kon vensjonen kommer til anvendelse vil siste setning i denne artikkel bli fortolket slik: 4. In trades to which Article 3 of the Code of Conduct applies, the last sentence of that Article is interpreted as meaning that: a) De to grupper nasjonale linjer skal samordne sine synspunkter før de stemmer over spørsmål angående trafikken mellom deres to land (a) the two groups of national shipping lines will co-ordinate their positions before voting on matters concerning the trade be tween their two countries; b) Denne setning gjelder bare spørsmål som etter konferanseavtalen bare kan av gjøres med samtykke fra begge berørte grup per av nasjonale linjer, og ikke alle spørsmål som dekkes av konferanseavtalen. (b) this sentence applies solely to matters which the conference agreement identifies as requiring the assent of both groups of national shipping lines concerned, and not to all matters covered by the conference agreement. Annex II To Council Regulation (EEC) No. 954/79 Vedlegg 11. Megling som omtalt i artikkel 3.3 Conciliation referred to in Article 3 (3) The parties to the dispute shall designate one or more coniliators. Partene i tvisten skal oppnevne en eller flere meglingsmenn.

to in Article 3 (3) The parties to the dispute shall designate one or more coniliators. Partene i tvisten skal oppnevne en eller flere meglingsmenn.

side 137

Vedlegg 4 Should they f ail to agree on the matter, each of the parties to the dispute shall desig nate a conciliator and the conciliators thus designated shall co-opt another conciliator to act as chairman. Should a party fail to designate a conciliator or the conciliators designated by the parties fail to reach agree ment on the chairman, the President of the International Chamber of Commerce shall, at the request of one of the parties, take the necessary disignations. Blir ikke partene enige i dette spørsmål, skal hver av partene i tvisten utnevne en meglingsmann, og de meglingsmenn som på denne måte utpekes, velger i fellesskap yt terligere en meglingsmann som skal fungere som formann. Hvis en part ikke utpeker en meglingsmann, eller de meglingsmenn som partene har oppnevnt ikke blir enige om valg av formann, foretar presidenten i Det internasjonale handelskammer, på begjæ ring fra en av partene, de nødvendige opp nevnelser. The conciliators shall make every endea vour to settle the dispute. They shall decide on the procedure to be followed. Their fees shall be paid by the parties to the dispute. Meglingsmennene skal gjøre sitt beste for å bilegge tvisten. De fastsetter seiv saksbe handlingen. Deres honorar dekkes av partene.

side 138

Vedlegg 6 Note om «A» -punkter fra De faste repræsentanters Komité til Rådet i De Europæiske Fællesskaber Vedr.: Vedtagelse på fællesskabssprogene af Rådets forordning om medlemssta ternes ratifikation eller tiltrædelse af De forenede Nationers konvention om en kodeks for linjekonferencer. Rådet gav på sin 578. samling den 8. maj 1979 sin tilslutning til indholdet af oven nævnte forordning og til optagelse af erklæ ringerne herom i sin mødeprotokol; det på lagde komiteen at udarbejde betragtnin gerne og at foranledige forordningen gjen nemgået i juristlingvistgruppen med hen blikk på endelig vedtagelse som «A» -punkt på Rådets næste samling. Da udarbejdelsen af betragtningerne og gennemgangen i juristlingvistgruppen er blevet foretaget efter de sædvanlige proce dure vedtog De faste repræsentanters Ko mité på sit 942. møde den 11. maj 1979 at henstille til Rådet — at det formelt på fællesskabssprogene og som «A» -punkt på sin neste samling ved tager denne forordning, således som den foreligger i dok. 6645/79 MAR 28 RELEX 31 + Corr 1 (i) 2 (d) 3 (e); — at det giver sin tilslutning til, at erklæringerne i b i 1 a g e t dette dokument op tages i mødeprotokollen for denne samling. Bilag Erklceringer til optagelse i Rådets mødeproto kol for den samling, hvorunder forordningen vedtages formelt. 1. Rådet erklærer: Når medlemsstaterne træffer foranstalt ninger i henhold til artikel 2, stk. 2, i Rådets forordning om medlemsstaternes ratifika tion eller tiltrædelse af De forenede Natio ners konvention om en kodeks for linjekon ferencer, anvender de følgende kriterier: a) den mængde last, der transporteres af konferencen, og som hidrører fra de medlemsstater, hvis trafik udføres af konfe rencen; b) det omfang i hvilket den indsatte skibska pacitet svarer til den nuværende eller for ventede lastmængde på den eller de linjer, der besejles af konferencen; c) den sandsynlige virkning, som rederiets medlemskab af konferencen vil få på ef fektiviteten og kvaliteten af konferencens tjenesteydelser; d) ansøgerens nuværende deltagelse i trafik på samme eller konkurrerende linje(r) udenfor eller indenfor den konference, som der søges medlemskab af; e) hvorvidt ansøgerens forretningsmæssige grundlag er foreneligt med det, der er etableret i overensstemmelse med markedsøkonomiske principper; f) den lastandel, der i den pågældende fart transporteres eller vil blive transporteret af de nationale rederier ved regelmæssig anvendelse af befragtede skibe. Kriterierne i første afsnit foregriber ikke en senere definition af begrebet nationalt rederi. 2. Under henvisning til de almindelige handelsmæssige principper i forordningens artikel 3 erkender Råd e t, at det er vigtigt at undgå alvorlige og pludselige forstyrrel ser af søfarten. Rådet har vedtaget denne erklæring for at tåge rimeligt hensyn til de f ragtskabende landes interesser. Samtidig med at den danske dele gation tilsluttede sig denne erklæring, meddelte den, at den for sit vedkommende ikke fortolkede den således, at hensigten dermed skulle være at ændre ligevækten mellem de forskellige handelsmessige prin cipper, der skal tages hensyn til ved den i artikel 3 i forordningen omhandlede omfor deling af lasten. Den belgiske delegation har godkendt for ordningen og især artikel 3, fordi kriteriet om «den mængde last, der transporteres af konferencen, og som frembringes af den medlemsstat, hvis handel betjenes af konfe rencen» i forbindelse med vurderingen af en rimelig omfordeling, er nævnt i litra a) blandt de i artikel 3, stk. 2, nævnte kriterier. 3. Rådet og Kommissionen vedtager, at så hurtigt som muligt efter vedtagelsen af for ordningen iværksætter medlemsstaterne og Kommissionen indenfor rammerne af OECD en aktion med henblik på at tilskynde de øvrige OECD-medlemslande, som deltagere i kodeks, til at vedtage tilsvarende løsninger, især for så vidt angår artikel 2, artikel 14, stk. 9, og artikel 25 i kodeks. Ved videreførel sen af denne aktion og under hensyntagen

løsninger, især for så vidt angår artikel 2, artikel 14, stk. 9, og artikel 25 i kodeks. Ved videreførel sen af denne aktion og under hensyntagen

side 139

Vedlegg 6 til de øvrige OECD-landes eventuelle hold ninger kan Fællesskabet på anmodning af en medlemsstat eller af Kommissionen om nødvendigt undersøge situationen indenfor rammerne af konsultationsproceduren; Rå det er navnlig af den opfatelse, at deltagel sen af alle industrilandenes linjer i konfe rencetrafiken mellem OECD-staterne bør foregå på kommerciel basis. om en kodeks for linjekonferencer efter in ternational ret gælder for trafiken mellem de lande, der er kontraherende parter deri. Den italienske delegation tilslutter sig denne erklæring, som den fortolker således, at medlemsstaterne dermed stilles helt frit med hensyn til deres søfartspolitik overfor stater, der ikke har tiltrådt kodeks-konven tionen, med forbehold af anvendelsen af be stemmelserne i denne forordning, af over holdelsen af EØF-traktaten og alle andre fællesskabsbestemmelser samt af overhol delsen af eventuelle forpligtelser til anden side. 4. Rådet vedtager, at så snart en medlems stat bliver kontraherende part i konventio nen om en kodeks for linjekonferencer, de ponerer denne stat i overensstemmelse med denne kodeks' artikel 51 en begæring om ændring deraf med henblik på Fællesskabets tiltrædelse af denne konvention. Når de øv rige medlemsstater bliver parter i kodeks, støtter de denne ændring. Så snart denne ændring er trådt i kraft, foreslår Kommis sionen Rådet at deponere det dokument, hvorved Fællesskabet tiltræder konventio nen; Rådet træffer afgørelse om dette for slag snarest muligt. Under henvisning til den italienske erklæ ring erklærer den danske delega tion og Det forenede kongeriges delegation, at Fællesskabet og dets medlemsstater fortsat bør tilrettelægge de res søfartspolitik efter liberale principper. 7. Rådet og Kommissionen er af den opfat telse, at kodeks for linjekonferencer er et eksempel på blandet kompetence mellem Fellesskabet og medlemsstaterne. 5. Rådet opfordrer i givet fald Kommissio nen til i god tid inden revisionskonferencen og på baggrund af opnåede erfaringer at forelægge et forslag om ændring af kodeks. 8. For så vidt angår artikel 1 i forordnin gen gør Det forenede kongeriges delegation opmærksom på sin holdning til spørgsmålet om ratif ikationen af Den eu ropæiske overenskomst om arbejde, der ud føres af det kørende personale i internati onal Vejtransport (AETR) (jf- dok. R/2940/77 (TRANS 203)) og meddelte, at Det forenede kongeriges tiltrædelse af De forenede Natio ners konvention om en kodeks for linjekon ferencer ville kræve, at Det forenede konge riges parlament i forvejen vedtager en na tional lovgivning. Kommissionen erklærer, at den vil fore lægge Rådet et sådant forslag, som bl. a. kunne vedrøre: a) bestemmelserne om fragtrater og bilæggelse af uoverensstemmelser; b) konkurrencen fra rederier, der ikke er medlemmer af en linjekonference; c) bilaterale farter på grundlag af regeringsaf taler; 6. Rådet og Kommissionen er af den opfat telse, at De forenede Nationers konvention

Vedlegg 7 Introductory Note from: Permanent Representatives Committee to: Coun cil European Communities Subject: Preparation for the Council meeting on transport on 7 June 1983 — Proposal for a Council Regulation laying down detailed niles for the application of Articles 85 and 86 of the Treaty to maritime transport I. Introduction. 1. The Commission submitted the above proposal for a Regulation to the Council on 16 October 1981. 2. The Economic and Social Committee de li vered its Opinion on 27 January 1983 1 ). The European Parliament has not yet delivered its Opinion. 2. The aim of the proposal for a Regula tion is to specify the arrangements for app lying the rules on competition in the Treaty to maritime transport. The main object is to give liner conferences a group exemption from the prohibition on restrictive practices in Article 85 of the Treaty, provided that certain essential conditions and certain ob ligations are fulfilled and that external cir cumstances are such as to permit healthy competition. The proposal also gives the Commission power to investigate and im pose fines where Article 85(*) is infringed or a dominant position is abused. 3. The Regulation is intended to supple ment Regulation (EEC) No 954/79 concer ning the ratification by Member States of, or their accession to, the United Nations Convention on a Code of Conduct for Liner Conferences 2 ). The recitals in the latter Regulation sta ted that the stabilizing role of conferences in ensuring reliable services to shippers was recognized, but stated it was nevertheless necessary to avoid possible breaches by con ferences of the rules of competition laid down in the treaty. They provided that the Commission would accordingby forward to the Council a proposal for a Regulation con cerning the application of those rules to sea transport. '). CES 73/83. 2 ) OJ No L 121, 17.5.1979, p.l. 4. Since the Convention on the Code of Conduct for Liner Conferences and the pro visions relating thereto in Regulation (EEC) No 954/79 are to enter into force on 6 Octo ber 1983, following the deposit of instru ments of ratification or accession by Ger many and the Netherlands, most dele gat i o n s felt that, in connection with the finalization of the Regulation under discus sion, consensus should be reached as soon as possible on the broad lines of provisions go verning the group exemption of cargo liner conferences. 5. The Permanent Representatives Com mittee therefore examined a draft Council Resolution which is attached for infor mation. Agreement was reached on a large part of this draft. It nevertheless emerged from the Permanent Representatives Comdiscussion that there were funda mental differences of opinion on certain points. The Permanent Representatives Commit tee therefore felt that it would be premature to sumbit the draft for Council approval. It nevertheless considered it advisable to ac quaint the Council with the points of diffe rence set out in section 11. 1 to 3 below so that a policy debate could take place if ne cessary. 11. Main points on which there are differences of opinion 1. Possibility of implementing the rules on competition in the Treaty in the field of maritime transport. The Italian delegation felt that the legal basis for the proposal for a Regulation could not be Article 87 of the Treaty but would have to be Article 84(2). The Council should therefore adopt appropriate provisions on maritime transport policy on the basis of Article 84(2) before applying the rules on competition in the Treaty to such transport. The Italian delegation further clarified its position on this matter in 6464/83 MAR 25 RELEX 46. Neither the other delegations nor the Com mission representative shared the Italian deview. They felt that the Commu nity rules on competition should be drawn up now and that consideration of the proside 141

shared the Italian deview. They felt that the Commu nity rules on competition should be drawn up now and that consideration of the proside 141

Vedlegg 7 blem of the legal basis for the Regulation should be postponed to a later stage when the final content of the draft Regulation had become clearer. The Council states that the following gui delines inter alia should be followed in dis cussion of the proposal for a Regulation laying down detailed rules for the applica tion of Articles 85 and 86 of the Treaty to maritime transport, without prejudice to its legal basis and to the regime to be applied to passenger services. 2. Inclusion in the Regulation of provisions on access to conferences for new companies. The Greek delegation considered that the Regulation should oblige conferences not to place restrictions on the admission of new shipping companies except where such rest rictions were indispensable in order to achieve the objectives of group exemption as set out in Article 85(3) of the Treaty. I. To the extent that liner conferences and consortia agreements generally comply with the four criteria of Article 85(3) of the Treaty, including/the [presence of actual or potential competition with] 2 ) other trans portere, there will be a group exemption of conference and consortia agreements from the prohibition [mentioned in Article 850) of the Treaty] ( 3 ): The other delegations and the Commission representative were uncertain as to what ex actly this request would entail. They wanted to ensure that the proposed provisions would under no circumstances prevent the formation of closed conferences. (a) subject to the condition or obligation of non-discrimination between the same goods according to the country of origin or destination on the same traffic link; In their view, there was nothing in the competition rules in the Treaty to prevent the formation of such closed conferences. Moreover, their existence was recognized in the Code of Conduct for Liner Conferences. (b) subject to obligations for: — consultations between shippers and conferences, 3. Exclusion of cases in which competition is — the publication of tariffs, eliminated by measures or practices adop- — the drawing up of loyalty agree ments 4 ); ted by third countries. The French delegation wanted no provi sion in the Regulation for withdrawing the group exemption for conferences when com petition had been eliminated as a result of measures or practices adopted by the go vernments of third countries involving, in particular, the exclusion of non-conference companies. This request was all the more justified in that in this case the conferences had had no part in creating the situation. — the free choice of the inland transporter, [— the elimination of restrictions on conference membership insofar as such restrictions are not indispen sable to the attainment of the objec tives aimed at by the exemption,] 5 ) (c) subject to the other obligations which may be 8 ) necessary to comply with the requirements of Article 85( 3 ); The other delegations and the Commission representative considered that, since under Article 85(3) of the Treaty the existence of actual or potential competition was a prere quisite for the grant of group exemption, the elimination of competition through measu res or practices, whether or not adopted by the State, would require examination of the appropriate steps to be taken to remedy the situation and a review of the legal position of the conference. (d) subject to the examination of whether there should also be an obligation to re frain from acting in such a way as to eliminate competition; (e) with, in case of non-compliance with the obligations: — a recommendation to those involved, 2 ) The French delegation proposes that this read: «the requirement not to eliminate the actual or potential competition of». 3 ) Reservation pending examination by the Ger man delegation. Annex. Draft Council Resolution on the Proposal for a Council Regulation laying down detailed rules for the application of articles 85 and 86 of the Treaty to maritime transport'). *) The Greek delegation wished to add the follo wing phrase: «ensuring that no elimination of competition occurs in respect of substantial parts of trade». 5 ) Addition proposed by the Greek delegation. The other delegations thought that such an ob ligation could not be derived from the competi tion rules of the Treaty. Om lov om linjekonferanser m.v. — if the recommendation is not respecditions in accordance with the provisions of the Treaty. ted: a procedure for imposing fines and examination of the need for more severe remedies up to withdrawal «ex Special attention should be given to the following circumstances: nunc» of the group exemption. — elimination of competition due to measu res or practices of third countries; II. 1 ) — elimination of competition due to preda tory practices by a conference. In cases where there is no actual or poten tial competition, it must be examined which appropriate measures should be taken to re medy this situation. Such measures should be flexible enough to take into account the specific characteristics of each case, with the aim of re-establishing competitive con111. There shall also be provision for group exemption for agreements, decisions and concerted practices between transport users, or between them or their associations on the one hand, and liner conferences on the other, concerning the quality, rates and conditions of liner services. ') Reservation by the French delegation, which thought it was not appropriate to provide for the creation of the «two-strength system» advo cated by the majority, according to which, in addition to the sanctions, further measures should be tanken to remedy the situation in cases where competition was absent. IV. In accordance with the provisions of Ar ticle 86 of the Treaty, particular attention should be given to situations where the abuse of a dominant position by a confe rence can affect viable access to trades. The Italian delegation entered a reservation pending examination. The German delegation stated that point II of the Resolution did not prejudge the question of whether measures should be taken pursuant to the rules on competition and, if so, what measu res, or whether measures should be taken in another context. If such cases occur, measures should be taken to remedy the situation.
